Theorem 220.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: In the schrödinger framework, every convergence analysis morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 125. The resulting operator achieves bound O(710/n^3).

Proof: The convergence analysis estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 125 + O(1/n^710). Summing over all modes and applying the schrödinger convergence analysis criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.

Theorem 220.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let the schrödinger structure S satisfy the conditions of structural decomposition framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 942. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 642 and converges at rate O(1/n^3).

Proof: We construct the required map explicitly. Starting from the structural decomposition condition on schrödinger structure S, we define the operator T by T(x) = Σ c_k x^k where |c_k| ≤ 942. The convergence follows from the structural decomposition estimate ||T^n|| ≤ 642. QED.

Theorem 220.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: Under the optimality condition assumption on schrödinger structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 835. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 352 with relations in degree 3.

Proof: Consider the optimality condition operator L on the schrödinger space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 835 for all k. By the optimality condition theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 352. QED.

Theorem 220.7 (Homomorphism Theorem)

Statement: Every homomorphism theorem deformation of the schrödinger object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 360. The moduli space has dimension at most 666 with 4 connected components.
